Vedic <note>This term indicates that the manuscript belongs to the tradition of the Vedas, the oldest and most sacred scriptures of India. In this context, it likely refers to "Jyotisha," one of the six Vedangas or "limbs" of Vedic study.</note>
</margin>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the astronomical data from the previous section. While the preceding page focused on the daily motion and velocity of the Moon (Soma), this page shifts to a tabular tool for determining the Ascendant (Lagna)—the specific point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at a given moment. This is a crucial calculation in both Indian astronomy and astrology for casting horoscopes and determining auspicious times.</meta>

# Auspicious K. S. <note>original: "श्री क. स." (Shri Ka. Sa.), likely an abbreviation for the title of the astronomical treatise or the author's name.</note>

<warning>The right edge of the manuscript is damaged, leading to partial loss of characters at the end of many horizontal lines in the table.</warning>

->**Salutation to the holy Ganesha. The first sections of the ascensional differences <note>original: "pala-chara-khandas." These are units used to adjust the rising times of zodiac signs based on the observer's latitude.</note> for calculating the shadow. A tool for calculating the Ascendant sheet. Line 25.**<-

48

<note>This page contains a highly technical "Lagnapatrasadhani" table. The headers represent the first six zodiac signs (Aries through Virgo). The rows of numbers likely represent "palas" and "vipalas"—traditional Indian units of time where 60 vipalas make 1 pala, and 60 palas make 1 ghati (24 minutes). These tables allow a mathematician to find the precise rising time of a sign for a specific geographic location.</note>

### Planetary Attributes and Calculations
<note>original: "Graha kā gha ce guṇa śra vā kā bhī." These are abbreviations likely referring to the planetary motions and properties (Guna) required for the chart.</note>

| Sun | Moon | Mercury | Rahu | Mars | Jupiter | Ketu | Venus | Saturn |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 5 | 4 | 7 | 17 | 27 |
| 9 | 24 | 24 | 2 | 32 | 5 | 26 | 25 | 52 |
| 42 | 49 | 45 | 50 | 32 | 37 | 27 | 2 | 52 |
| 11 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

---

<center>
[Astrological Diagram: A square North Indian style chart (Kundali) divided into twelve triangular houses, used to map planetary positions at the moment of birth.]
</center>

Now the method for calculating the <term>Chakra</term> <note>the astrological wheel or chart</note> is stated. Multiply the <term>Palas</term> <note>a unit of time/arc equal to 24 seconds</note> by 60. Divide those <term>Anshas</term> <note>degrees</note> by 30 to determine the zodiac signs. From these twelve <term>Rashis</term> <note>zodiac signs</note>, subtract one to find the transition point... this is the rule for the formation of the chart.

---

### Calculations from the Grahalaghava System
<note>original: "Graha-nā-gha-ve," a likely shorthand for the Grahalaghava, a standard 16th-century astronomical text used for calculating planetary positions without complex trigonometry.</note>

| Sun | Moon | Mercury | Rahu | Mars | Jupiter | Ketu | Venus | Saturn |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | 11 | 5 | 0 | 10 | 8 | 7 | 7 | 8 |
| 19 | 12 | 7 | 4 | 7 | 29 | 2 | 20 | 29 |
| 42 | 6 | 33 | 39 | 8 | 33 | 26 | 8 | 29 |
| 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

</center>

<term>Moon (Soma)</term>: The central celestial body for this table.
<term>Year (Varsha)</term>: The time units used for these calculations.
<term>Table (Kosthaka)</term>: The mathematical grid used to organize astronomical data.
<term>Almanac (Panchanga)</term>: A traditional Hindu calendar used to find auspicious times based on planetary positions.
<term>Astronomy/Astrology (Jyotisha)</term>: The traditional Indian science of celestial observation.
<term>Mathematics (Ganita)</term>: The calculative aspect of astronomy.
<term>Lunar Mansion (Nakshatra)</term>: One of the 27 sectors of the ecliptic through which the Moon passes.

<summary>This page presents mathematical tables for determining the position and motion of the Moon (Soma) across different years of the Shaka era (1520–1621). The data is organized into columns likely representing the 27 Nakshatras (lunar mansions) to aid in precise astronomical or astrological forecasting.</summary>
<keywords>The Moon, Soma, Shaka Era, astronomical tables, lunar motion, Nakshatra, Jyotisha, Indian mathematics</keywords>

<note>This manuscript page contains an astronomical grid, commonly known as a *Panchang* or Ephemeris. The numerical values likely represent the daily motion, positions, or degree corrections for various planets. Each table corresponds to a specific year in the Shaka calendar. The abbreviations on the right identify which planet the row of data pertains to.</note>

<summary>This page features a dense astronomical ephemeris for the Shaka years 1538–1541 (1616–1619 CE), presenting coordinated numerical data for the Moon, Sun, and planets. It serves as a practical reference for determining celestial positions during this four-year period.</summary>

<term>Mercury (Budha)</term>: In Indian astronomy, Mercury is associated with communication and intellect. Its swift motion requires complex tables to track its various phases of visibility.
<term>Nakshatras</term>: A system of 27 "Lunar Mansions" or star clusters along the ecliptic. Each column represents one of these segments: 1. Ashwini, 2. Bharani, 3. Krittika, 4. Rohini, 5. Mrigashira, 6. Ardra, 7. Punarvasu, 8. Pushya, 9. Ashlesha, 10. Magha, 11. Purva Phalguni, 12. Uttara Phalguni, 13. Hasta, 14. Chitra, 15. Swati, 16. Vishakha, 17. Anuradha, 18. Jyeshtha, 19. Mula, 20. Purva Ashadha, 21. Uttara Ashadha, 22. Shravana, 23. Dhanishta, 24. Shatabhisha, 25. Purva Bhadrapada, 26. Uttara Bhadrapada, 27. Revati.

<note>Glossary of technical terms: 
**Rising (Udaya):** The moment Mercury becomes visible after being obscured by the sun.
**Setting (Asta):** The moment Mercury becomes invisible as it nears the sun.
**Retrograde (Vakra):** The period when Mercury appears to move backward in the sky.
**Direct (Margi):** The resumption of normal forward motion.
**Latitude correction (Akshama):** A calculation used to adjust for the observer's latitude when determining the visibility of planets.</note>

<keywords>Mercury, Budha, Nakshatra, Retrograde, Planetary Motion, Shaka Era, Indian Astronomy, Ephemeris</keywords>

<note>
Technical Vocabulary:
- Mercury (Budhaḥ): The target planet of this ephemeris.
- Astronomy (Jyotiṣa): The science used to compile these tables.
- Table (Koṣṭhaka): The grid format used for calculation.
- Rising (Udaya): The heliacal rising, when the planet first becomes visible before sunrise.
- Setting (Upa): Short for *upaplava* or *uparyasta*, referring to the planet's heliacal setting or disappearance from the night sky.
- Direct motion (Mā): Short for *mārga*, indicating the planet is moving in its normal direction rather than retrograde.
- Lunar Mansion (Nakṣatra): The 27 divisions of the sky used as a coordinate system.
</note>

The table tracks the planet's longitudinal progress, its motion (direct or retrograde), and its visibility (rising or setting).</meta>

<note>Vocabulary used in this manuscript:
**Budha**: The planet Mercury.
**Nakshatra**: One of the 27 sectors of the ecliptic (Lunar Mansions).
**Vikram Samvat**: A historical Hindu calendar era beginning in 57 BCE.
**Upa**: Correction or proximate value.
**Va**: Retrograde (Vakra).
**Madhyama (Ma)**: Mean/average position.
**Mu**: Likely stands for *Mukta* (released), indicating the end of a specific planetary phase or retrograde period.
**Vaga**: Retrograde motion (*Vakragati*).
</note>

<note>
The table lists coordinates in sexagesimal format (base-60), which was the standard for medieval and early modern Indian astronomy. The rows alternate between the planet's position (given in zodiacal signs, degrees, and minutes) and its "Gati" or daily speed. The 27 columns likely represent a mathematical division of the orbital cycle, often linked to the 27 lunar mansions or "Nakshatras."
</note>

<summary>This page contains a comprehensive astronomical grid tracking the positions and daily motions of the Sun, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars across 27 cycles or lunar mansions. The numerical data provide precise coordinates used for calculating the Hindu calendar and astrological charts.</summary>
<keywords>astronomical table, Sun (Ravi), Jupiter (Guru), Saturn (Shani), Mars (Mangala), Gati (motion), Nakshatra, sexagesimal, Hindu astronomy</keywords>

<note>Glossary of technical terms on this page:
**Saka Era**: A historical calendar system widely used in Indian astronomy.
**Table (Koshtaka)**: The grid structure used to organize planetary longitudinal data.
**Astronomy (Jyotisha)**: The science of observing celestial bodies.
**Mathematics (Ganita)**: The calculation methods used to derive positions.
**Lunar Mansion (Nakshatra)**: One of the 27 divisions of the sky along the ecliptic.
**Motion (Gati)**: The rate of movement of a planet, often recorded in degrees per unit of time.</note>

<summary>This page contains a highly structured ephemeris for Venus, using the Saka era for its chronological framework. It features a 26-column table of astronomical coordinates and sexagesimal calculations likely used to predict planetary positions across different lunar mansions.</summary>

<keywords>Saka Era, Venus, Nakshatra, astronomical table, sexagesimal, planetary motion, Indian astronomy, ephemeris</keywords>

<note>This table serves as an ephemeris, a tool used by astronomers and astrologers to predict the positions of celestial bodies. The numbers across the top (1-27) represent the Nakshatras, or Lunar Mansions, which are segments of the sky that the Moon passes through each month. Each row tracks how the planets Jupiter, Saturn, and Rahu move through these segments over the course of four years. The use of three different calendar systems in the margins—Vikrama Samvat, Shaka Samvat, and a third likely representing the Hijri year—was common in Indian manuscripts to ensure the data could be used across different cultural and administrative contexts.</note>

<summary>This page contains a numerical astronomical table tracking the movements of Jupiter, Saturn, and Rahu through the 27 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) for the years 1542–1545 of the Vikrama Samvat era. It illustrates the high degree of mathematical precision in medieval Indian calendrical science and the use of multiple concurrent dating eras.</summary>

->**The Instrument for Attaining Success through the Houses**<-
<note>original: **भावाप्रसिद्धि यंत्र** (*Bhavaprasiddhi Yantra*). A *Yantra* in this context is a mathematical table or manual for calculating the twelve astrological houses (*Bhavas*).</note>

The values are presented in the sexagesimal system (base-60), typical of the Indian astronomical tradition known as Jyotisha.</meta>

<note>
The row labels (A, M, G) are abbreviations for specific astronomical terms. 
**A** (Adya/Amsha) generally refers to the initial degree or degree-based position.
**M** (Madhya) refers to the mean longitude or position of the sun.
**G** (Gati/Gatika) refers to the daily motion or elapsed progress of the planet.
Each cell contains three values, likely representing Degrees, Minutes, and Seconds.
</note>

<note>Terminology:
- <term>Signs (Rashi)</term>: One of the 12 divisions of the zodiac (30 degrees each).
- <term>Degrees (Ansha)</term>: Standard units of circular measure.
- <term>Minutes (Kala)</term>: 1/60th of a degree.
- <term>Seconds (Vikala)</term>: 1/60th of a minute.
- <term>Thirds (Prativikala)</term>: 1/60th of a second, used for high-precision calculations.</note>

<note>This table serves as an astronomical ephemeris for Saturn, the "Slow-Moving One." Because Saturn takes approximately 29.5 years to complete a single orbit around the Sun, its yearly change in position is small—about 12 to 13 degrees, as seen in the "Degree" row. The table provides precision down to the "Fourth" (Pratitatparā), which is 1/1,296,000th of a degree, illustrating the sophisticated level of mathematical calculation in medieval Indian astronomy.</note>

<meta>This page transitions from specific lunar node (Rahu) calculations to a general Ascendant Table, known as a Lagnapatra. This table is used by astrologers to determine which zodiac sign is rising on the eastern horizon at a given time and date.</meta>

<warning>The manuscript is damaged on the left edge with significant loss of text in the first few columns. The ink is faded or smudged in some areas. The paper is frayed and has some staining. The text consists of a dense mathematical or astrological grid in Devanagari numerals.</warning>

### Ascendant Table

<note>A Lagnapatra is a specialized table used in Vedic astrology to calculate the rising sign (ascendant). The values in the cells are expressed in the traditional format of Signs, Degrees, Minutes, and Seconds. The signs are numbered 0 through 11, where 0 represents Aries and 11 represents Pisces.</note>

<term>Lagnapatra (Ascendant Table): A mathematical tool for determining the point of the ecliptic that rises on the eastern horizon at a specific time.</term>
<term>Signs: The 12 divisions of the zodiac (Rashi), here numbered 0-11.</term>
<term>Degrees, Minutes, Seconds: Units of arc (Amsha, Kala, Vikala) used to specify precise celestial positions.</term>

->**Salutation to the Illustrious <term>Ganesha</term> [the elephant-headed god of beginnings and remover of obstacles]; Salutation to the Illustrious <term>Saraswati</term> [the goddess of learning and wisdom]; Salutation to the Illustrious Krishna, [my] refuge.**<-

<note>original: "श्रीगणेशाय नमः श्रीसरस्वत्यै नमः श्रीकृष्णाय नमः शरणं" — These opening invocations, known as Mangalacharana, are traditional in Sanskrit manuscripts to ensure the successful completion of the work without hindrance.</note>

<warning>The bottom edge of the manuscript is significantly frayed and damaged. There is some minor foxing and a dark stain in the lower-left section of the gridded area.</warning>

### Ascendant Table

<note>This table is a Lagnapatra, used by Vedic astrologers to find the rising sign. The top row (5 through 30) likely represents the day of the month or a specific degree of the Sun. Each cell contains coordinates in the format of **Sign-Degree** (top row) and **Minute-Second** (bottom row). Signs are numbered 0 to 11: 0=Aries, 1=Taurus, 2=Gemini, 3=Cancer, 4=Leo, 5=Virgo, 6=Libra, 7=Scorpio, 8=Sagittarius, 9=Capricorn, 10=Aquarius, 11=Pisces.</note>
